Bash: cat -n R/locate.R | head -60 
cat -n R/locate.R | head -60
     1	#' Locate the maximum or minimum along an axis
     2	#'
     3	#' @description
     4	#' - `rray_locate_max()` finds the position of the maximum along `axis`.
     5	#'
     6	#' - `rray_locate_min()` finds the position of the minimum along `axis`.
     7	#'
     8	#' @details
     9	#' The dimensionality of `x` is retained in the result, with `axis` collapsed
    10	#' to size 1.
    11	#'
    12	#' Ties return the first position, like [which.max()] and [which.min()]:
    13	#'
    14	#' ```r
    15	#' rray_locate_max(c(1, 3, 3), 1L) # 2
    16	#' ```
    17	#'
    18	#' Missing values win, so the position of the first one is returned. `NA` wins
    19	#' over `NaN`, like [rray_max()] and [rray_min()]:
    20	#'
    21	#' ```r
    22	#' rray_locate_max(c(1, NaN, NA), 1L) # 3
    23	#' rray_locate_max(c(1, NaN, NA), 1L, na_rm = TRUE) # 1
    24	#' ```
    25	#'
    26	#' When there is no position to return, the result is `NA`. This happens when
    27	#' `axis` has dimension 0, or when every value is missing and `na_rm = TRUE`:
    28	#'
    29	#' ```r
    30	#' rray_locate_max(c(NA, NA), 1L, na_rm = TRUE) # NA
    31	#' ```
    32	#'
    33	#' @inheritParams rlang::args_dots_empty
    34	#'
    35	#' @param x An array.
    36	#'
    37	#' @param axis A single integer giving the axis to locate along. `1` locates
    38	#'   along rows, `2` along columns, and so on.
    39	#'
    40	#' @param na_rm If `TRUE`, missing values are skipped.
    41	#'
    42	#' @returns
    43	#' An integer array with the same dimensionality as `x`, but with the dimension
    44	#' along `axis` reduced to 1.
    45	#'
    46	#' @name rray-locate
    47	#' @examples
    48	#' x <- array(c(3L, 1L, 2L, 4L, 6L, 5L), c(3L, 2L))
    49	#'
    50	#' # Position of the maximum in each column
    51	#' rray_locate_max(x, 1L)
    52	#'
    53	#' # Position of the minimum in each row
    54	#' rray_locate_min(x, 2L)
    55	NULL
    56	
    57	#' @rdname rray-locate
    58	#' @export
    59	rray_locate_max <- function(x, axis, ..., na_rm = FALSE) {
    60	  check_dots_empty0(...)

Waiting on you: R/locate.R:18-19

Your comment:

Change "Missing values win" to "Missing values are infectious". Should we return NA when we can't figure out the minimum or maximum due to missing values? Consistent with empty axis? Discuss with me locally

The current docs:

#' Missing values win, so the position of the first one is returned. `NA` wins
#' over `NaN`, like [rray_max()] and [rray_min()]:
#'
#' rray_locate_max(c(1, NaN, NA), 1L) # 3

The choice is what rray_locate_max(c(1, NaN, NA), 1L) should return:

  • Keep returning the missing value's position (3). This mirrors rray_max(), which returns the missing value itself. The position always points at the element that rray_max() would return. You also learn where the problem is. The downside is that 3 looks like a real answer, so callers have to check whether it points at a missing value.

  • Return NA. This treats "can't tell because of missing values" the same as "nothing to find" (an empty axis, or all values missing with na_rm = TRUE). There is then one simple rule: NA means no answer. The downside is that you lose the location of the missing value, and the result no longer lines up with rray_max().

I lean towards returning NA. A position should name a real maximum, and having a single "no answer" value is easier to work with. Note that which.max() doesn't help settle this, because it always skips missing values. If we switch, the NA vs NaN ordering stops mattering, which removes that paragraph and simplifies the C kernels.

Still to do after that

  • R/locate.R:37-38: shorten the axis param.

  • R/locate.R:50 and :53: reword the two example comments.

  • src/locate.c:224: remove the special handling for position 1 by filling best and out from the first element before the loop. This depends on the missing-value decision, since an NA answer changes how missing values are handled in that loop.

Which way do you want to go on missing values?

Oct 7, 2026, 10:14 UTC

Davis

Return NA and simplify the C kernels as much as possible accordingly (you can treat them as if you are writing them for the first time, since we are!)

Claude

#define RRAY_LOCATE(CTYPE, CONST_DEREF, DEREF, IS_MISSING, ONE)                \
  const CTYPE* v_x = CONST_DEREF(x);                                           \
  CTYPE* v_best = DEREF(best);                                                 \
                                                                               \
  for (; !rray_run_iterator_done(it); rray_run_iterator_next2(it)) {           \
    r_ssize start = rray_run_iterator_start(it);                               \
    const r_ssize end = rray_run_iterator_end(it);                             \
                                                                               \
    r_ssize out_loc = rray_run_iterator_loc(it, 0);                            \
    const r_ssize out_stride = rray_run_iterator_stride(it, 0);                \
                                                                               \
    int position = (int) rray_run_iterator_loc(it, 1) + 1;                     \
    const int position_stride = (int) rray_run_iterator_stride(it, 1);         \
                                                                               \
    if (out_stride == 0) {                                                     \
      if (position == 1) {                                                     \
        v_best[out_loc] = v_x[start];                                          \
        v_out[out_loc] = 1;                                                    \
        ++start;                                                               \
        position += position_stride;                                           \
      }                                                                        \
                                                                               \
      CTYPE best_elt = v_best[out_loc];                                        \
      int best_position = v_out[out_loc];                                      \
                                                                               \
      for (r_ssize i = start; i < end; ++i) {                                  \
        const CTYPE x_elt = v_x[i];                                            \
        const bool is_better = ONE(x_elt, best_elt);                           \
        best_elt = is_better ? x_elt : best_elt;                               \
        best_position = is_better ? position : best_position;                  \
        position += position_stride;                                           \
      }                                                                        \
                                                                               \
      v_best[out_loc] = best_elt;                                              \
      v_out[out_loc] = best_position;                                          \
    } else if (position == 1) {                                                \
      for (r_ssize i = start; i < end; ++i) {                                  \
        v_best[out_loc] = v_x[i];                                              \
        v_out[out_loc] = 1;                                                    \
        out_loc += out_stride;                                                 \
      }                                                                        \
    } else {                                                                   \
      for (r_ssize i = start; i < end; ++i) {                                  \
        const CTYPE x_elt = v_x[i];                                            \
        const bool is_better = ONE(x_elt, v_best[out_loc]);                    \
        v_best[out_loc] = is_better ? x_elt : v_best[out_loc];                 \
        v_out[out_loc] = is_better ? position : v_out[out_loc];                \
        out_loc += out_stride;                                                 \
      }                                                                        \
    }                                                                          \
  }                                                                            \
                                                                               \
  if (na_rm) {                                                                 \
    const r_ssize out_size = r_length(best);                                   \
                                                                               \
    for (r_ssize i = 0; i < out_size; ++i) {                                   \
      v_out[i] = IS_MISSING(v_best[i]) ? r_globals.na_int : v_out[i];          \
    }                                                                          \
  }

static void rray_locate_max_lgl(
  r_obj* x,
  r_obj* best,
  bool na_rm,
  struct rray_run_iterator* it,
  int* v_out
) {
  if (na_rm) {
    RRAY_LOCATE(
      int,
      r_lgl_cbegin,
      r_lgl_begin,
      rray_lgl_is_missing,
      rray_locate_max_lgl_one_na_rm
    );
  } else {
    RRAY_LOCATE(
      int,
      r_lgl_cbegin,
      r_lgl_begin,
      rray_lgl_is_missing,
      rray_locate_max_lgl_one
    );
  }
}

static rray_locate_fn rray_locate_switch(
  r_obj* x,
  enum rray_locate_op op,
  struct rray_arg* arg,
  struct r_lazy error_call
) {
  switch (rray_typeof(x)) {
  case RRAY_TYPE_logical:
    switch (op) {
    case RRAY_LOCATE_max:
      return rray_locate_max_lgl;
    case RRAY_LOCATE_min:
      return rray_locate_min_lgl;
    }
  case RRAY_TYPE_integer:
    switch (op) {
    case RRAY_LOCATE_max:
      return rray_locate_max_int;
    case RRAY_LOCATE_min:
      return rray_locate_min_int;
    }
  case RRAY_TYPE_double:
    switch (op) {
    case RRAY_LOCATE_max:
      return rray_locate_max_dbl;
    case RRAY_LOCATE_min:
      return rray_locate_min_dbl;
    }

  case RRAY_TYPE_complex:
  case RRAY_TYPE_character:
  case RRAY_TYPE_raw:
  case RRAY_TYPE_list:
    switch (op) {
    case RRAY_LOCATE_max:
      stop_unsupported_locate("maximum", x, arg, error_call);
    case RRAY_LOCATE_min:
      stop_unsupported_locate("minimum", x, arg, error_call);
    }

  case RRAY_TYPE_scalar:
    stop_scalar_input(x, arg, error_call);
  }

  r_stop_unreachable();
}

static r_no_return void stop_unsupported_locate(
  const char* op,
  r_obj* x,
  struct rray_arg* arg,
  struct r_lazy error_call
) {
locate_oracle <- function(x, axis, fn, na_rm) {
  lane <- function(values) {
    if (!na_rm) {
      missing <- which(is.na(values) & !is.nan(values))
      nan <- which(is.nan(values))
      if (length(missing)) {
        return(missing[[1L]])
      }
      if (length(nan)) {
        return(nan[[1L]])
      }
    }

    out <- fn(values)
    if (length(out)) out else NA_integer_
  }

  dimensions <- dim(x)
  out_dimensions <- dimensions
  out_dimensions[[axis]] <- 1L
  margins <- setdiff(seq_along(dimensions), axis)

  if (length(margins) == 0L) {
    return(array(lane(x), out_dimensions))
  }

  array(apply(x, margins, lane), out_dimensions)
}

r_obj* ffi_rray_max(
  r_obj* ffi_x,
  r_obj* ffi_axes,
  r_obj* ffi_na_rm,
  r_obj* ffi_frame
) {
  struct r_lazy error_call = {.x = ffi_frame, .env = r_null};
  const bool na_rm = r_arg_as_bool(ffi_na_rm, "na_rm");
  return rray_max(ffi_x, ffi_axes, na_rm, rray_args.x, error_call);
}

r_obj* ffi_rray_min(
  r_obj* ffi_x,
  r_obj* ffi_axes,
  r_obj* ffi_na_rm,
  r_obj* ffi_frame
) {
  struct r_lazy error_call = {.x = ffi_frame, .env = r_null};
  const bool na_rm = r_arg_as_bool(ffi_na_rm, "na_rm");
  return rray_min(ffi_x, ffi_axes, na_rm, rray_args.x, error_call);
}

r_obj* rray_max(
  r_obj* x,
  r_obj* axes,
  bool na_rm,
  struct rray_arg* arg,
  struct r_lazy error_call
) {
  return rray_reduce(x, axes, na_rm, rray_max_switch, arg, error_call);
}

r_obj* rray_min(
  r_obj* x,
  r_obj* axes,
  bool na_rm,
  struct rray_arg* arg,
  struct r_lazy error_call
) {
  return rray_reduce(x, axes, na_rm, rray_min_switch, arg, error_call);
}

static rray_reduce_fn rray_max_switch(
  r_obj* x,
  struct rray_arg* arg,
  struct r_lazy error_call
) {
  switch (rray_typeof(x)) {
  case RRAY_TYPE_logical:
    return rray_max_lgl;
  case RRAY_TYPE_integer:
    return rray_max_int;
  case RRAY_TYPE_double:
    return rray_max_dbl;

  case RRAY_TYPE_complex:
  case RRAY_TYPE_character:
  case RRAY_TYPE_raw:
  case RRAY_TYPE_list:
    stop_unsupported_reduce("maximum", x, arg, error_call);

  case RRAY_TYPE_scalar:
    stop_scalar_input(x, arg, error_call);
  }

  r_stop_unreachable();
}

static rray_reduce_fn rray_min_switch(
  r_obj* x,
  struct rray_arg* arg,
  struct r_lazy error_call
) {
  switch (rray_typeof(x)) {
  case RRAY_TYPE_logical:
    return rray_min_lgl;
  case RRAY_TYPE_integer:
    return rray_min_int;
  case RRAY_TYPE_double:
    return rray_min_dbl;

  case RRAY_TYPE_complex:
  case RRAY_TYPE_character:
  case RRAY_TYPE_raw:
  case RRAY_TYPE_list:
    stop_unsupported_reduce("minimum", x, arg, error_call);

  case RRAY_TYPE_scalar:
    stop_scalar_input(x, arg, error_call);
  }

  r_stop_unreachable();
}

static r_obj* rray_max_lgl(
  r_obj* x,
  bool na_rm,
  r_ssize out_size,
  const int* v_dimensions,
  int dimensionality,
  const r_ssize* v_out_broadcast_strides,
  struct r_lazy error_call
) {

    RRAY_REDUCE(
      int,

      R_TYPE_logical,
      int,

      0,
      rray_max_lgl_one_na_rm

  } else {
    RRAY_REDUCE(
      int,
      r_lgl_cbegin,
      R_TYPE_logical,
      int,
      r_lgl_begin,
      0,
      rray_max_lgl_one
    );
  }
}

      r_int_begin,
      -INT_MAX,
      rray_max_int_one
    );
  }
}

  return !rray_lgl_is_missing(best) && (rray_lgl_is_missing(x) || x > best);

static inline bool rray_locate_max_lgl_one_na_rm(int x, int best) {

static inline bool rray_locate_max_int_one(int x, int best) {
  return !rray_int_is_missing(best) && (rray_int_is_missing(x) || x > best);
}

static inline bool rray_locate_max_int_one_na_rm(int x, int best) {
  return x > best;
}

static inline bool rray_locate_max_dbl_one(double x, double best) {
  return rray_dbl_is_missing(x) || x > best;
}

static inline bool rray_locate_max_dbl_one_na_rm(double x, double best) {
  return !rray_dbl_is_missing(x) && !(x <= best);
}

static inline bool rray_locate_min_lgl_one(int x, int best) {
  return x < best;
}

static inline bool rray_locate_min_lgl_one_na_rm(int x, int best) {
  return !rray_lgl_is_missing(x) && (rray_lgl_is_missing(best) || x < best);
}

static inline bool rray_locate_min_int_one(int x, int best) {
  return x < best;
}

static inline bool rray_locate_min_int_one_na_rm(int x, int best) {
  return !rray_int_is_missing(x) && (rray_int_is_missing(best) || x < best);
}

static inline bool rray_locate_min_dbl_one(double x, double best) {
  return rray_dbl_is_missing(x) || x < best;
}

static inline bool rray_locate_min_dbl_one_na_rm(double x, double best) {
  return !rray_dbl_is_missing(x) && !(x >= best);
}
'''
open(p,'w').write(s)

static inline void* r_vec_begin0(enum r_type type, r_obj* x) {
    switch (type) {
    case R_TYPE_logical:
        return r_lgl_begin(x);
    case R_TYPE_integer:
        return r_int_begin(x);
    case R_TYPE_double:
        return r_dbl_begin(x);
    case R_TYPE_complex:
        return r_cpl_begin(x);
    case R_TYPE_raw:
        return r_raw_begin(x);
    default:
        r_stop_unimplemented_type(type);
    }
}
static inline void* r_vec_begin(r_obj* x) {
    return r_vec_begin0(r_typeof(x), x);
}

static inline const void* r_vec_cbegin0(enum r_type type, r_obj* x) {
    switch (type) {
    case R_TYPE_logical:
        return r_lgl_cbegin(x);
    case R_TYPE_integer:
        return r_int_cbegin(x);
    case R_TYPE_double:
        return r_dbl_cbegin(x);
    case R_TYPE_complex:
        return r_cpl_cbegin(x);
    case R_TYPE_raw:
        return r_raw_cbegin(x);
    case R_TYPE_character:
        return r_chr_cbegin(x);
    case R_TYPE_list:
        return r_list_cbegin(x);
    default:
        r_stop_unimplemented_type(type);
    }
}
static inline const void* r_vec_cbegin(r_obj* x) {
    return r_vec_cbegin0(r_typeof(x), x);
}

223	    const int position_stride = (int) rray_run_iterator_stride(it, 1);         \
224	                                                                               \
225	    if (out_stride == 0) {                                                     \

239	        best_elt = is_better ? x_elt : best_elt;                               \
240	        best_position = is_better ? position : best_position;                  \
241	        position += position_stride;                                           \

Thinking

Pre-filling with memcpy() slows short-lane cases due to an extra pass over x, so instead I'll adopt rray_reduce()'s approach: initialize best to the identity value (or NA when na_rm=TRUE) and every position to 1, avoiding the extra read entirely.

150	static rray_locate_fn rray_locate_switch(
151	  r_obj* x,
152	  enum rray_locate_op op,
153	  struct rray_arg* arg,
154	  struct r_lazy error_call
155	) {
156	  switch (rray_typeof(x)) {
157	  case RRAY_TYPE_logical:
158	    switch (op) {
159	    case RRAY_LOCATE_max:
160	      return rray_locate_max_lgl;
161	    case RRAY_LOCATE_min:
162	      return rray_locate_min_lgl;
163	    }
164	  case RRAY_TYPE_integer:
165	    switch (op) {
166	    case RRAY_LOCATE_max:
167	      return rray_locate_max_int;
168	    case RRAY_LOCATE_min:
169	      return rray_locate_min_int;
170	    }
171	  case RRAY_TYPE_double:
172	    switch (op) {
173	    case RRAY_LOCATE_max:
174	      return rray_locate_max_dbl;
175	    case RRAY_LOCATE_min:
176	      return rray_locate_min_dbl;
177	    }
178	
179	  case RRAY_TYPE_complex:
180	  case RRAY_TYPE_character:
181	  case RRAY_TYPE_raw:
182	  case RRAY_TYPE_list:
183	    switch (op) {
184	    case RRAY_LOCATE_max:
185	      stop_unsupported_locate("maximum", x, arg, error_call);
186	    case RRAY_LOCATE_min:
187	      stop_unsupported_locate("minimum", x, arg, error_call);
188	    }
189	
190	  case RRAY_TYPE_scalar:
191	    stop_scalar_input(x, arg, error_call);
192	  }
193	
194	  r_stop_unreachable();
195	}

211	static void rray_locate_init(
212	  r_obj* x,
213	  r_obj* best,
214	  int* v_out,
215	  const int* v_x_dimensions,
216	  int dimensionality,
217	  int axis
218	) {
219	  const r_ssize inner_size =
220	    rray_size_from_dimensions(v_x_dimensions, axis - 1);
221	  const r_ssize outer_size =
222	    rray_size_from_dimensions(v_x_dimensions + axis, dimensionality - axis);
223	  const r_ssize dimension = v_x_dimensions[axis - 1];
224	
225	  const r_ssize best_chunk_size = inner_size * r_vec_elt_sizeof(x);
226	  const r_ssize x_chunk_size = best_chunk_size * dimension;
227	
228	  const unsigned char* v_x = r_vec_cbegin(x);
229	  unsigned char* v_best = r_vec_begin(best);
230	
231	  for (r_ssize i = 0; i < outer_size; ++i) {
232	    r_memcpy(v_best, v_x, best_chunk_size);
233	    v_best += best_chunk_size;
234	    v_x += x_chunk_size;
235	  }
236	
237	  const r_ssize out_size = inner_size * outer_size;
238	
239	  for (r_ssize i = 0; i < out_size; ++i) {
240	    v_out[i] = 1;
241	  }
242	}

The a1 case still runs about 10% slower due to writing starting values in a separate pass, but everything else, including i1, is within noise. Since the simpler code is worth that tradeoff, I'll commit it.

All 6 review threads on PR #134 are fixed, pushed and resolved. The full test suite passes.

Missing values now give NA (R/locate.R:18-19, src/locate.c). I used your docs wording. https://github.com/DavisVaughan/rray4/commit/d858e9dd7050f53ede83f554fbf703b52f0bc59b

  • Any NA or NaN along axis now gives NA. The final pass that turns a missing best into NA now runs in both modes, not only with na_rm = TRUE.

  • The double comparisons drop the NA vs NaN ranking. They are now one line each: rray_dbl_is_missing(x) || x > best. Once best is missing the answer is NA anyway, so it doesn't matter which missing value it holds.

  • The min comparisons for logical and integer become just x < best. The old check for a missing best did nothing, because NA is the smallest int and nothing compares below it.

  • Tests: the test oracle (the plain R reference used to check results) now returns NA for any missing value. I replaced the "first missing value" and "NA wins over NaN" tests with one "missing values give NA" test. The rray_max() comparison now also checks that both return NA on the same lanes.

No more special case for position 1 (src/locate.c:224). https://github.com/DavisVaughan/rray4/commit/ced79d9e8be51dcf8d20a727741f79664011e361

I didn't fill best from the first element as you suggested. Copying the first slice is an extra pass over x. On 10 x 1e6 inputs along axis 1, it was about 25% slower for doubles and 55% slower for integers with na_rm = TRUE.

Instead each kernel takes a fixed starting value, the same way RRAY_REDUCE() does, and every position starts at 1:

  • With na_rm = FALSE, best starts at the identity (-Inf, -INT_MAX or FALSE for max). If every value equals the identity, the strict > never fires and position 1 is kept, which is correct.

  • With na_rm = TRUE, best starts at NA. Every na_rm comparison already lets the first real value replace a missing best. If all values are missing, the final pass gives NA.

I added the // Algorithm requires at least 1 input to run comment above the early exit for dimension 0, as you asked in the review. It's the only comment I added.

Performance: one case is about 10% slower (10 x 1e6 doubles along axis 1, 13.6 ms before and 15.0 ms after). The cause is the extra pass that writes the starting values. Every other shape I measured is within noise.

Docs

  • R/locate.R:9-10: "collapsed to a dimension of 1". https://github.com/DavisVaughan/rray4/commit/0b1b885893ff32ae0e5310f4334b9b983ea8fe06

  • R/locate.R:37-38: shorter axis param. https://github.com/DavisVaughan/rray4/commit/d8c257353191b4d118c9c6cccd8012613d9290e0

  • R/locate.R:50: "going down the rows". https://github.com/DavisVaughan/rray4/commit/352a2990ca17281abdabf6a956413dcb8fa9bafe

  • R/locate.R:53: "going across the columns". https://github.com/DavisVaughan/rray4/commit/006ce39d0c02723cf67cffa3f10583f261f6b1e9

The editor errors are only because the worktree has no compile_commands.json. Now the benchmark script:

Write /tmp/bench-locate-bitwise.R 
args <- commandArgs(TRUE)
path <- args[[1]]
label <- args[[2]]
suppressMessages(pkgload::load_all(path, quiet = TRUE))

set.seed(1)
n <- 1e7

make <- function(type, missing) {
  x <- switch(
    type,
    dbl = runif(n),
    int = sample.int(1e6, n, TRUE),
    lgl = sample(c(TRUE, FALSE), n, TRUE)
  )
  if (missing) {
    x[sample.int(n, n / 10)] <- NA
  }
  x
}

shapes <- list(
  vec_ax1 = list(dim = n, axis = 1L),
  short_ax1 = list(dim = c(10L, 1e6L), axis = 1L),
  long_ax1 = list(dim = c(1e6L, 10L), axis = 1L),
  short_ax2 = list(dim = c(1e6L, 10L), axis = 2L),
  long_ax2 = list(dim = c(10L, 1e6L), axis = 2L),
  square_ax1 = list(dim = c(1e3L, 1e4L), axis = 1L),
  square_ax2 = list(dim = c(1e3L, 1e4L), axis = 2L),
  cube_ax2 = list(dim = c(100L, 1000L, 100L), axis = 2L)
)

rows <- list()

for (type in c("dbl", "int", "lgl")) {
  for (missing in c(FALSE, TRUE)) {
    x <- make(type, missing)
    for (shape in names(shapes)) {
      s <- shapes[[shape]]
      dim(x) <- s$dim
      for (op in c("max", "min")) {
        fn <- if (op == "max") rray_locate_max else rray_locate_min
        for (na_rm in c(FALSE, TRUE)) {
          res <- bench::mark(
            fn(x, s$axis, na_rm = na_rm),
            min_iterations = 10,
            max_iterations = 30,
            check = FALSE,
            filter_gc = FALSE
          )
          rows[[length(rows) + 1L]] <- data.frame(
            build = label,
            type = type,
            missing = missing,
            shape = shape,
            op = op,
            na_rm = na_rm,
            median_ms = as.numeric(res$median) * 1000
          )
        }
      }
    }
  }
}

out <- do.call(rbind, rows)
write.csv(out, args[[3]], row.names = FALSE)

Write /tmp/compare-locate-bitwise.R 
read <- function(label) {
  files <- Sys.glob(sprintf("/tmp/locate-%s-*.csv", label))
  runs <- lapply(files, read.csv)
  out <- runs[[1]]
  out$median_ms <- do.call(pmin, lapply(runs, `[[`, "median_ms"))
  out$build <- NULL
  out
}

logical <- read("logical")
bitwise <- read("bitwise")
keys <- c("type", "missing", "shape", "op", "na_rm")
both <- merge(logical, bitwise, by = keys, suffixes = c("_logical", "_bitwise"))
both$ratio <- both$median_ms_bitwise / both$median_ms_logical

changed <- with(
  both,
  (op == "max" & !na_rm & type != "dbl") |
    (op == "max" & na_rm & type == "dbl") |
    (op == "min" & na_rm) |
    (op == "min" & !na_rm & type == "dbl") |
    (op == "max" & !na_rm & type == "dbl")
)
both$changed <- changed

both <- both[order(both$ratio), ]
options(width = 200)
print(both, row.names = FALSE, digits = 3)

cat("\nGeometric mean ratio (bitwise / logical)\n")
agg <- aggregate(
  ratio ~ changed + type + missing,
  both,
  function(r) exp(mean(log(r)))
)
print(agg, digits = 3)

Control functions shifted by up to 1.7x, suggesting code layout noise rather than real changes. I'll rebuild both copies with -falign-loops=64 before drawing any conclusions.

Bash: cat ~/.claude/projects/-Users-davis-files-r-packages-rray4/memory/project_loop_ali… 
cat ~/.claude/projects/-Users-davis-files-r-packages-rray4/memory/project_loop_alignment_noise.md ~/.claude/projects/-Users-davis-files-r-packages-rray4/memory/project_benchmarking_against_main.md; cat ~/files/r/packages/rray4/src/Makevars
---
name: project-loop-alignment-noise
description: "Hot loop placement in the .so can swing rray4 benchmarks by up to 1.36x, rule it out with -falign-loops=64 before blaming a code change"
metadata:
  node_type: memory
  type: project
  originSessionId: d2aac6a7-43e9-467c-b320-d21c596687ec
  modified: 2026-10-06T22:44:35.466Z
---

On 2026-10-06, building the run iterator inside each `rray_locate_*()` kernel instead of in `rray_locate()` looked up to 1.36x slower (int, axis 1, `na_rm = TRUE`) and 0.75x faster in another case. The hot loops were identical instruction for instruction. Rebuilding both versions with `CFLAGS=-O2 -falign-loops=64` (through `R_MAKEVARS_USER`) brought every case to within 1%.

**Why:** Apple arm64 cores are sensitive to where a tight loop lands, and any unrelated change can move it.

**How to apply:** when a benchmark gap is consistent but the hot loop's asm (`otool -tvV`) is the same, rebuild both sides with `-falign-loops=64` before deciding anything. See [[project-benchmarking-against-main]] and [[project-second-caller-breaks-inlining]].
---
name: project-benchmarking-against-main
description: "How to benchmark a branch against main in rray4, load_all() builds at -O2 and single runs are noisy"
metadata:
  node_type: memory

`devtools::load_all()` compiles at `-O2` here, because `~/.R/Makevars` sets `CFLAGS=-O2`, so it is fine for benchmarks. To compare against main, use `git worktree add --detach /tmp/rray4-main main` and run the same `bench/*.R` script from both roots, alternating between them for 3 rounds.

**Why:** with 20 iterations, single cases on 16M-element binary ops swung from 0.64x to 1.40x by noise alone. Rerunning with 60 iterations brought every one to within about 2%.

**How to apply:** treat any single-case gap above about 10% as unconfirmed until a focused rerun with more iterations backs it up.

Picking the loop out of 800 lines is error-prone, so I'll compile just the inner loop on its own in both forms.

Write /tmp/locate-loop.c 
#include <limits.h>
#include <stdbool.h>
#include <stddef.h>

static inline bool is_missing(int x) {
  return x == INT_MIN;
}

static inline bool bool_bitwise_or(bool x, bool y) {
  return (int) x | (int) y;
}

static inline bool bool_bitwise_and(bool x, bool y) {
  return (int) x & (int) y;
}

static inline bool one_logical(int x, int best) {
  return !is_missing(best) && (is_missing(x) || x > best);
}

static inline bool one_bitwise(int x, int best) {
  return bool_bitwise_and(
    !is_missing(best),
    bool_bitwise_or(is_missing(x), x > best)
  );
}

#define LOOP(ONE)                                                              \
  int best_elt = *p_best;                                                      \
  int best_position = *p_position;                                             \
  for (ptrdiff_t i = start; i < end; ++i) {                                    \
    const int x_elt = v_x[i];                                                  \
    const bool is_better = ONE(x_elt, best_elt);                               \
    best_elt = is_better ? x_elt : best_elt;                                   \
    best_position = is_better ? position : best_position;                      \
    position += position_stride;                                               \
  }                                                                            \
  *p_best = best_elt;                                                          \
  *p_position = best_position;

void loop_logical(
  const int* v_x,
  ptrdiff_t start,
  ptrdiff_t end,
  int position,
  int position_stride,
  int* p_best,
  int* p_position
) {
  LOOP(one_logical)
}

void loop_bitwise(
  const int* v_x,
  ptrdiff_t start,
  ptrdiff_t end,
  int position,
  int position_stride,
  int* p_best,
  int* p_position
) {
  LOOP(one_bitwise)
}

Thinking

The gap comes from && letting clang short-circuit on the first missing best, skipping the rest of the loop, while the bitwise version always evaluates every element—neither vectorizes though. I'll wait for the aligned benchmark rerun now.

---
name: project-locate-bitwise-slower
description: "Bitwise and/or helpers make the locate one functions slower (int/lgl up to 5.8x), keep && and || there"
metadata:
  type: project
---

On 2026-10-07, swapping `&&` / `||` for `bool_bitwise_and()` / `bool_bitwise_or()` in the `rray_locate_*_one()` functions in `src/locate.c` was benchmarked on 192 cases with `-falign-loops=64`. Doubles were unchanged. Int and logical were 1.18x slower with no missing values and 1.5x slower with 10% missing, up to 5.8x for max with `na_rm = FALSE` along axis 1.

**Why:** the locate loops never vectorize, because they also track a position. With `&&`, clang keeps a well predicted branch on "`best` is missing", and once a lane hits `NA` the loop stops loading `x`. Bitwise forces every check onto every element.

**How to apply:** bitwise helpers only pay off in loops that vectorize, like the reducers in `one-extremum.h`. Don't add them to argmax style loops. See [[project-loop-alignment-noise]].

No, the bitwise helpers don't help. Doubles don't change, and integer and logical get slower, by up to 5.8x. I'd keep && and || as they are on the branch now.

Setup

  • I made a copy of the branch where the 8 comparisons that use && / || call bool_bitwise_and() / bool_bitwise_or() instead. The other 4 comparisons (x > best, x < best) are the same in both copies, so I used them as controls.

  • I ran 192 cases: double, integer and logical, with no missing values or 10% missing, 8 shapes, max and min, na_rm on and off.

  • The shapes: a 1e7 vector, 10 x 1e6 and 1e6 x 10 along each axis, 1e3 x 1e4 along each axis, and a 100 x 1000 x 100 cube along axis 2.

  • Each case ran twice per copy, and I kept the faster median.

  • The first round was noisy: the controls moved by up to 1.7x even though their code was identical. So I rebuilt both copies with -falign-loops=64, as your benchmarking notes suggest. After that the controls averaged within 1% of each other.

Results with aligned loops (geometric mean of bitwise / current, comparisons I changed)

Type No missing 10% missing
double 1.00 1.00
integer 1.18 1.51
logical 1.20 1.52

The worst cases:

  • Max with na_rm = FALSE along axis 1, with missing values: 3.0 ms becomes 17.5 ms (5.8x).

  • Min with na_rm = TRUE along axis 1: 9.3 ms becomes 15.4 ms (1.67x).

Why

These loops never vectorize, because they also track the position of the best value. In that case bitwise just does more work per element. I compiled the integer max loop on its own to check:

  • With &&, clang keeps a branch on "best is missing". Before the first NA it always goes one way, and the CPU predicts it perfectly.

  • Once a lane hits its first NA, the branch flips and the loop stops even loading x. It just counts to the end of the lane. That is where the 5.8x comes from.

  • With bitwise, both checks run on every element, and that adds work on the path that updates best.

The bitwise helpers in one-extremum.h make sense because those reducer loops vectorize, so avoiding branches matters there. The position tracking in locate rules that out.

Clean up

  • I removed the temporary worktree. I rebuilt nothing in the repo, but I cleared the compiled code so your next load_all() builds without the alignment flag.

  • I left /tmp/rray4-bitwise alone. It was already there from an earlier session and isn't a git worktree.

  • I saved this result to memory so I don't suggest bitwise helpers in loops like these again.

Davis

And there’s no obvious way to make them vectorize?

# Faster stride 0 loops for min and max

## Summary

Reducing over an axis that is contiguous in memory gives runs where every
element goes to the same output location (`out_stride == 0`). For several
reducers that loop is scalar and runs 5 to 10 times slower than the other
branch. The fix is to write the loop so clang can vectorize it.

Everything below was measured on an M2 Mac with Apple clang 17 at `-O2`,
16M element inputs, fastest of 3 alternating rounds against `main`.

## What blocks vectorization

clang only vectorizes a loop that carries a value across iterations if it
recognizes that value as a reduction (a sum, a min, a max, an or). Each of
these breaks that:

- A select that is not a plain min or max. `has_na ? NA : max(out, x)` is
  not recognized, so int max without `na_rm` stays scalar.

- Floating point compare and select. `out < x ? x : out` on doubles is only
  vectorized with `-ffast-math`, because of `NaN` and signed zero. There is
  no pragma to allow it for a single loop. `fmax()` does not help either,
  clang 17 keeps it scalar.

- A `uint8_t` running value. `missing < x_missing ? x_missing : missing`
  promotes to `int` and truncates back, and the reduction is not recognized.
  An `int` or `int64_t` running value vectorizes.

`clang -O2 -Rpass-analysis=loop-vectorize` names the reason for each loop,
for example "value that could not be identified as reduction is used outside
the loop".

The other branch (`out_stride != 0`) does not have this problem, since each
lane updates a different output. clang emits a SIMD version guarded by a
runtime overlap check.

## Keeping the running value in a local

`RRAY_REDUCE` wrote `v_out[out_loc]` on every iteration of the stride 0 loop.
Reading it into a local before the loop and writing it back after (as
`rray_sum_dbl()` already does) is a small win.

```c
OUT_CTYPE out_elt = v_out[out_loc];

for (r_ssize i = start; i < end; ++i) {
  out_elt = ONE(out_elt, v_x[i]);
}

v_out[out_loc] = out_elt;
```

- Most reducers: 0.97 to 1.00. The loop is limited by each step waiting on
  the previous one, and store to load forwarding hides most of the store.

- prod double, `[200, 200, 200]` over 1, 3: 0.85.

- max double with `na_rm`, `[10, 1e6]` over 1: 0.64. Short runs gain the
  most.

Worth doing on its own.

## Which stride 0 loops are already SIMD

From the assembly of `reduce-extremum.c`:

- int max with `na_rm`: SIMD. `rray_max_int_one_na_rm()` is a plain max,
  since integer `NA` is `INT_MIN`.

- int min without `na_rm`: SIMD. A plain min already returns `NA`.

- int max without `na_rm`: scalar, 10 ms vs 1 ms.

- int min with `na_rm`: scalar, 9 ms vs 1 ms.

- double max and min, both `na_rm` settings: scalar.

The logical versions mirror the integer ones.

## Integer max and min

Write the stride 0 loop as a plain max or min plus a separate missing flag.

Max without `na_rm`:

```c
int out_elt = v_out[out_loc];
bool any_missing = rray_int_is_missing(out_elt);

for (r_ssize i = start; i < end; ++i) {
  const int x_elt = v_x[i];
  out_elt = out_elt < x_elt ? x_elt : out_elt;
  any_missing = bool_bitwise_or(any_missing, rray_int_is_missing(x_elt));
}

v_out[out_loc] = any_missing ? r_globals.na_int : out_elt;
```

`any_missing` starts from the stored output, otherwise an `NA` from an
earlier run is lost, since `max(NA, x)` is `x`.

Min with `na_rm`:

```c
int x_elt = v_x[i];
x_elt = rray_int_is_missing(x_elt) ? INT_MAX : x_elt;
out_elt = x_elt < out_elt ? x_elt : out_elt;
```

Results:

| Case | Shape | main | new | ratio |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| max, `na_rm = FALSE` | `[4000, 4000]` over 1 | 10.06 | 1.03 | 0.10 |
| max, `na_rm = FALSE` | `[200, 200, 200]` over 1, 3 | 5.07 | 0.58 | 0.11 |
| max, `na_rm = FALSE` | `[10, 1e6]` over 1 | 6.29 | 3.55 | 0.56 |
| min, `na_rm = TRUE` | `[4000, 4000]` over 1 | 9.03 | 1.45 | 0.16 |
| min, `na_rm = TRUE` | `[200, 200, 200]` over 1, 3 | 4.51 | 0.84 | 0.19 |
| min, `na_rm = TRUE` | `[10, 1e6]` over 1 | 4.99 | 4.98 | 1.00 |

All other int max and min cases were 0.94 to 1.01. Results matched base R
exactly.

Only change the stride 0 loop. Rewriting `rray_min_int_one_na_rm()` itself
made the stride 0 loop fast but made the other branch 26% slower.

## Double max and min

### The int64 key

Read a double's bits as an `int64_t` and flip every bit except the sign for
negative numbers. Integer order then matches double order, and an integer
max vectorizes.

```c
static inline int64_t rray_dbl_key(double x) {
  int64_t bits;
  memcpy(&bits, &x, sizeof(bits));
  return bits ^ ((bits >> 63) & INT64_MAX);
}

static inline double rray_key_dbl(int64_t key) {
  const int64_t bits = key ^ ((key >> 63) & INT64_MAX);
  double out;
  memcpy(&out, &bits, sizeof(out));
  return out;
}
```

```
-Inf < -2.0 < -1.0 < -0.0 < 0.0 < 1.0 < 2.0 < Inf
```

The flip is its own inverse. `memcpy()` costs nothing once inlined. Detect
`NaN` with `isnan()` on the double, not on the bits, since clang emits a
single `fcmeq` and shares it with `rray_dbl_classify()`.

### Missing values

Follow `rray_sum_int()`: the main loop skips missing values and tracks them
per output in `v_missings`, then a fix-up loop writes `NA` or `NaN`. Doubles
have two kinds of missing and `NA` beats `NaN`, so `v_missings` holds a
`uint8_t` class instead of a `bool`. Reordering `enum rray_dbl_class` to
`number, nan, missing` lets a plain max pick the right one. Nothing depends
on the enum's numeric values.

Only use the key inside the stride 0 loop. Convert `v_out[out_loc]` to a key
when the run starts and back when it ends. The other branch keeps plain
doubles with `rray_max_dbl_one_na_rm()`, which skips `NaN` for free since
comparisons with `NaN` are false. This needs no key buffer and no extra
conversion pass.

Stride 0 without `na_rm`:

```c
int64_t out_key = rray_dbl_key(v_out[out_loc]);
int missing = v_missings[out_loc];

for (r_ssize i = start; i < end; ++i) {
  const double x_elt = v_x[i];
  const int x_missing = rray_dbl_classify(x_elt);
  const int64_t key = x_missing ? INT64_MIN : rray_dbl_key(x_elt);
  out_key = out_key < key ? key : out_key;
  missing = missing < x_missing ? x_missing : missing;
}

v_out[out_loc] = rray_key_dbl(out_key);
v_missings[out_loc] = missing;

10 more lines cut

Thinking

Double compare-and-select reductions won't vectorize without -ffast-math, and the int64 key trick is off the table, so any SIMD gains here apply only to integer/logical data and cross-lane branching. I'll prototype both approaches in a small C file to get real numbers.

Write /tmp/locate-vec.c 
#include <limits.h>
#include <stdbool.h>
#include <stddef.h>
#include <stdint.h>
#include <stdio.h>
#include <stdlib.h>
#include <time.h>

#define NA INT_MIN

static inline bool is_missing(int x) {
  return x == NA;
}

// Current: one pass, max without na_rm
__attribute__((noinline)) int lane_current(const int* x, ptrdiff_t n) {
  int best = -INT_MAX;
  int pos = 1;
  for (ptrdiff_t i = 0; i < n; ++i) {
    const int x_elt = x[i];
    const bool better = !is_missing(best) && (is_missing(x_elt) || x_elt > best);
    best = better ? x_elt : best;
    pos = better ? (int) i + 1 : pos;
  }
  return is_missing(best) ? NA : pos;
}

// Two pass: vectorized max plus missing flag, then find
__attribute__((noinline)) int lane_two_pass(const int* x, ptrdiff_t n) {
  int best = -INT_MAX;
  int any_missing = 0;
  for (ptrdiff_t i = 0; i < n; ++i) {
    const int x_elt = x[i];
    best = best < x_elt ? x_elt : best;
    any_missing |= is_missing(x_elt);
  }
  if (any_missing) {
    return NA;
  }
  for (ptrdiff_t i = 0; i < n; ++i) {
    if (x[i] == best) {
      return (int) i + 1;
    }
  }
  return NA;
}

// Current na_rm max
__attribute__((noinline)) int lane_current_na_rm(const int* x, ptrdiff_t n) {
  int best = NA;
  int pos = 1;
  for (ptrdiff_t i = 0; i < n; ++i) {
    const int x_elt = x[i];
    const bool better = x_elt > best;
    best = better ? x_elt : best;
    pos = better ? (int) i + 1 : pos;
  }
  return is_missing(best) ? NA : pos;
}

__attribute__((noinline)) int lane_two_pass_na_rm(const int* x, ptrdiff_t n) {
  int best = NA;
  for (ptrdiff_t i = 0; i < n; ++i) {
    const int x_elt = x[i];
    best = best < x_elt ? x_elt : best;
  }
  if (is_missing(best)) {
    return NA;
  }
  for (ptrdiff_t i = 0; i < n; ++i) {
    if (x[i] == best) {
      return (int) i + 1;
    }
  }
  return NA;
}

// Across lanes, as written today (runtime stride)
__attribute__((noinline)) void across_current(
  const int* x,
  ptrdiff_t n,
  int* v_best,
  int* v_out,
  ptrdiff_t out_stride,
  int position
) {
  ptrdiff_t out_loc = 0;
  for (ptrdiff_t i = 0; i < n; ++i) {
    const int x_elt = x[i];
    const bool better =
      !is_missing(v_best[out_loc]) && (is_missing(x_elt) || x_elt > v_best[out_loc]);
    v_best[out_loc] = better ? x_elt : v_best[out_loc];
    v_out[out_loc] = better ? position : v_out[out_loc];
    out_loc += out_stride;
  }
}

// Across lanes, unit stride
__attribute__((noinline)) void across_unit(
  const int* x,
  ptrdiff_t n,
  int* v_best,
  int* v_out,
  int position
) {
  for (ptrdiff_t i = 0; i < n; ++i) {
    const int x_elt = x[i];
    const int best = v_best[i];
    const bool better = !is_missing(best) && (is_missing(x_elt) || x_elt > best);
    v_best[i] = better ? x_elt : best;
    v_out[i] = better ? position : v_out[i];
  }
}

static double now(void) {
  struct timespec ts;
  clock_gettime(CLOCK_MONOTONIC, &ts);
  return ts.tv_sec * 1e3 + ts.tv_nsec / 1e6;
}

#define TIME(LABEL, EXPR)                                                      \
  do {                                                                         \
    double best_ms = 1e9;                                                      \
    int result = 0;                                                            \
    for (int rep = 0; rep < 20; ++rep) {                                       \
      const double t0 = now();                                                 \
      result += (EXPR);                                                        \
      const double t1 = now();                                                 \
      best_ms = t1 - t0 < best_ms ? t1 - t0 : best_ms;                         \
    }                                                                          \
    printf("%-40s %8.2f ms  (%d)\n", LABEL, best_ms, result / 20);             \
  } while (0)

int main(void) {
  const ptrdiff_t n = 10000000;
  int* x = malloc(n * sizeof(int));
  int* x_na = malloc(n * sizeof(int));
  srand(1);
  for (ptrdiff_t i = 0; i < n; ++i) {
    x[i] = rand() % 1000000;
    x_na[i] = rand() % 10 == 0 ? NA : x[i];
  }

  TIME("lane, no na, current", lane_current(x, n));
  TIME("lane, no na, two pass", lane_two_pass(x, n));
  TIME("lane, 10% na, current", lane_current(x_na, n));
  TIME("lane, 10% na, two pass", lane_two_pass(x_na, n));
  TIME("lane na_rm, 10% na, current", lane_current_na_rm(x_na, n));
  TIME("lane na_rm, 10% na, two pass", lane_two_pass_na_rm(x_na, n));

  const ptrdiff_t lanes = 1000000;
  const ptrdiff_t dimension = n / lanes;
  int* v_best = malloc(lanes * sizeof(int));
  int* v_out = malloc(lanes * sizeof(int));

  TIME("across 1e6 x 10, current", ({
    for (ptrdiff_t j = 0; j < lanes; ++j) { v_best[j] = -INT_MAX; v_out[j] = 1; }
    for (ptrdiff_t k = 0; k < dimension; ++k) {
      across_current(x + k * lanes, lanes, v_best, v_out, 1, (int) k + 1);
    }
    v_out[0];
  }));
  TIME("across 1e6 x 10, unit stride", ({
    for (ptrdiff_t j = 0; j < lanes; ++j) { v_best[j] = -INT_MAX; v_out[j] = 1; }
    for (ptrdiff_t k = 0; k < dimension; ++k) {
      across_unit(x + k * lanes, lanes, v_best, v_out, (int) k + 1);
    }
    v_out[0];
  }));

  return 0;
}

The two-pass approach (vectorized max + scalar search for first match) gives 6x speedup normally and 16x with missing values, but clang won't vectorize the across-lanes loop in either form. I'll dig into why its cost model rejects it.

Write /tmp/locate-across.c 
#include <limits.h>
#include <stdbool.h>
#include <stddef.h>
#include <stdio.h>
#include <stdlib.h>
#include <time.h>

#define NA INT_MIN

static inline bool is_missing(int x) {
  return x == NA;
}

static inline bool bool_bitwise_or(bool x, bool y) {
  return (int) x | (int) y;
}

static inline bool bool_bitwise_and(bool x, bool y) {
  return (int) x & (int) y;
}

__attribute__((noinline)) void across_logical(
  const int* x, ptrdiff_t n, int* v_best, int* v_out, int position
) {
  for (ptrdiff_t i = 0; i < n; ++i) {
    const int x_elt = x[i];
    const int best = v_best[i];
    const bool better = !is_missing(best) && (is_missing(x_elt) || x_elt > best);
    v_best[i] = better ? x_elt : best;
    v_out[i] = better ? position : v_out[i];
  }
}

__attribute__((noinline)) void across_bitwise(
  const int* x, ptrdiff_t n, int* v_best, int* v_out, int position
) {
  for (ptrdiff_t i = 0; i < n; ++i) {
    const int x_elt = x[i];
    const int best = v_best[i];
    const bool better = bool_bitwise_and(
      !is_missing(best),
      bool_bitwise_or(is_missing(x_elt), x_elt > best)
    );
    v_best[i] = better ? x_elt : best;
    v_out[i] = better ? position : v_out[i];
  }
}

__attribute__((noinline)) void across_plain(
  const int* x, ptrdiff_t n, int* v_best, int* v_out, int position
) {
  for (ptrdiff_t i = 0; i < n; ++i) {
    const int x_elt = x[i];
    const int best = v_best[i];
    const bool better = x_elt > best;
    v_best[i] = better ? x_elt : best;
    v_out[i] = better ? position : v_out[i];
  }
}

static double now(void) {
  struct timespec ts;
  clock_gettime(CLOCK_MONOTONIC, &ts);
  return ts.tv_sec * 1e3 + ts.tv_nsec / 1e6;
}

#define TIME(LABEL, FN, X)                                                     \
  do {                                                                         \
    double best_ms = 1e9;                                                      \
    for (int rep = 0; rep < 20; ++rep) {                                       \
      for (ptrdiff_t j = 0; j < lanes; ++j) {                                  \
        v_best[j] = -INT_MAX;                                                  \
        v_out[j] = 1;                                                          \
      }                                                                        \
      const double t0 = now();                                                 \
      for (ptrdiff_t k = 0; k < dimension; ++k) {                              \
        FN(X + k * lanes, lanes, v_best, v_out, (int) k + 1);                  \
      }                                                                        \
      const double t1 = now();                                                 \
      best_ms = t1 - t0 < best_ms ? t1 - t0 : best_ms;                         \
    }                                                                          \
    long check = 0;                                                            \
    for (ptrdiff_t j = 0; j < lanes; ++j) check += v_out[j];                   \
    printf("%-30s %8.2f ms  (%ld)\n", LABEL, best_ms, check);                  \
  } while (0)

int main(void) {
  const ptrdiff_t n = 10000000;
  const ptrdiff_t lanes = 1000000;
  const ptrdiff_t dimension = n / lanes;
  int* x = malloc(n * sizeof(int));
  int* x_na = malloc(n * sizeof(int));
  int* v_best = malloc(lanes * sizeof(int));
  int* v_out = malloc(lanes * sizeof(int));
  srand(1);
  for (ptrdiff_t i = 0; i < n; ++i) {
    x[i] = rand() % 1000000;
    x_na[i] = rand() % 10 == 0 ? NA : x[i];
  }

  TIME("logical, no na", across_logical, x);
  TIME("bitwise, no na", across_bitwise, x);
  TIME("plain, no na", across_plain, x);
  TIME("logical, 10% na", across_logical, x_na);
  TIME("bitwise, 10% na", across_bitwise, x_na);
  return 0;
}

Write /tmp/locate-dbl.c 
#include <math.h>
#include <stdbool.h>
#include <stddef.h>
#include <stdio.h>
#include <stdlib.h>
#include <time.h>

static inline bool bool_bitwise_or(bool x, bool y) {
  return (int) x | (int) y;
}

__attribute__((noinline)) void across_current(
  const double* x, ptrdiff_t n, double* v_best, int* v_out, int position
) {
  for (ptrdiff_t i = 0; i < n; ++i) {
    const double x_elt = x[i];
    const bool better = isnan(x_elt) || x_elt > v_best[i];
    v_best[i] = better ? x_elt : v_best[i];
    v_out[i] = better ? position : v_out[i];
  }
}

__attribute__((noinline)) void across_hoist_bitwise(
  const double* x, ptrdiff_t n, double* v_best, int* v_out, int position
) {
  for (ptrdiff_t i = 0; i < n; ++i) {
    const double x_elt = x[i];
    const double best = v_best[i];
    const int out = v_out[i];
    const bool better = bool_bitwise_or(isnan(x_elt), x_elt > best);
    v_best[i] = better ? x_elt : best;
    v_out[i] = better ? position : out;
  }
}

__attribute__((noinline)) int lane_current(const double* x, ptrdiff_t n) {
  double best = -INFINITY;
  int pos = 1;
  for (ptrdiff_t i = 0; i < n; ++i) {
    const double x_elt = x[i];
    const bool better = isnan(x_elt) || x_elt > best;
    best = better ? x_elt : best;
    pos = better ? (int) i + 1 : pos;
  }
  return isnan(best) ? -1 : pos;
}

__attribute__((noinline)) int lane_two_pass(const double* x, ptrdiff_t n) {
  double best = -INFINITY;
  int any_missing = 0;
  for (ptrdiff_t i = 0; i < n; ++i) {
    const double x_elt = x[i];
    best = best < x_elt ? x_elt : best;
    any_missing |= isnan(x_elt);
  }
  if (any_missing) {
    return -1;
  }
  for (ptrdiff_t i = 0; i < n; ++i) {
    if (x[i] == best) {
      return (int) i + 1;
    }
  }
  return -1;
}

static double now(void) {
  struct timespec ts;
  clock_gettime(CLOCK_MONOTONIC, &ts);
  return ts.tv_sec * 1e3 + ts.tv_nsec / 1e6;
}

int main(void) {
  const ptrdiff_t n = 10000000;
  const ptrdiff_t lanes = 1000000;
  const ptrdiff_t dimension = n / lanes;
  double* x = malloc(n * sizeof(double));
  double* v_best = malloc(lanes * sizeof(double));
  int* v_out = malloc(lanes * sizeof(int));
  srand(1);
  for (ptrdiff_t i = 0; i < n; ++i) {
    x[i] = rand() / (double) RAND_MAX;
  }

  void (*across[])(const double*, ptrdiff_t, double*, int*, int) = {
    across_current, across_hoist_bitwise
  };
  const char* across_labels[] = {"across current", "across hoist bitwise"};

  for (int f = 0; f < 2; ++f) {
    double best_ms = 1e9;
    for (int rep = 0; rep < 20; ++rep) {
      for (ptrdiff_t j = 0; j < lanes; ++j) {
        v_best[j] = -INFINITY;
        v_out[j] = 1;
      }
      const double t0 = now();
      for (ptrdiff_t k = 0; k < dimension; ++k) {
        across[f](x + k * lanes, lanes, v_best, v_out, (int) k + 1);
      }
      const double t1 = now();
      best_ms = t1 - t0 < best_ms ? t1 - t0 : best_ms;
    }
    long check = 0;
    for (ptrdiff_t j = 0; j < lanes; ++j) check += v_out[j];
    printf("%-25s %8.2f ms  (%ld)\n", across_labels[f], best_ms, check);
  }

  int (*lane[])(const double*, ptrdiff_t) = {lane_current, lane_two_pass};
  const char* lane_labels[] = {"lane current", "lane two pass"};

  for (int f = 0; f < 2; ++f) {
    double best_ms = 1e9;
    int result = 0;
    for (int rep = 0; rep < 20; ++rep) {
      const double t0 = now();
      result = lane[f](x, n);
      const double t1 = now();
      best_ms = t1 - t0 < best_ms ? t1 - t0 : best_ms;
    }
    printf("%-25s %8.2f ms  (%d)\n", lane_labels[f], best_ms, result);
  }
  return 0;
}

Doubles show the same speedup pattern: cross-lane goes from 22.8ms to 3.3ms with hoisted bitwise loads, and along-lane two-pass drops from 39ms to 12ms since a plain max has a shorter dependency chain than one tracking position. Before finalizing, I need to confirm that two-pass only works if a run with out_stride == 0 always covers a whole lane.

76://   coalesce (2 * 1 = 2, 4 * 2 = 8) into one dimension [40] with stride [1],
81://   Both coalesce completely, producing a [40] loop with array stride [1] and
86://   [1, 2, 0]. The adjacent axes 1 and 2 coalesce (2 * 1 = 2) into size 6,
97://   third axis also coalesces. The result is one dimension [12] with stride
116://   array coalesces to point dimensions [40], with array strides [1] and scalar
127://   [2, 3, 4] array coalesces to dimensions [2, 12], with array strides [1, 2]
136://   produces output strides [0, 1, 3], which coalesce to dimensions [2, 12]

sed -n 160,300p src/strided-iterator.h; grep -n "rray__run_iterator_axes_coalesce" -A45 src/strided-iterator.h | sed -n 50,110p
  // Strides for all `n` arrays, laid out axis-major in a flat array as
  // [dimensionality][n], allowing contiguous access when doing "next" calls.
  r_ssize v_strides[RRAY_MAX_DIMENSIONALITY * RRAY_MAX_INPUTS];
  r_ssize n;
};

static inline void rray_run_iterator_init(
  struct rray_run_iterator* it,
  const int* v_dimensions,
  int dimensionality,
  const r_ssize* const* v_v_strides,
  r_ssize n
) {
  check_dimensionality(dimensionality);

  if (n < 1 || n > RRAY_MAX_INPUTS) {
    r_stop_internal(
      "`n` (%" R_PRI_SSIZE ") must be between 1 and %d.",
      n,
      RRAY_MAX_INPUTS
    );
  }

  it->size = rray_size_from_dimensions_checked(
    v_dimensions,
    dimensionality,
    r_lazy_null
  );

  for (int axis = 0; axis < dimensionality; ++axis) {
    it->v_dimensions[axis] = (r_ssize) v_dimensions[axis];
  }

  // Reorder strides to be contiguous during next() calls
  for (int axis = 0; axis < dimensionality; ++axis) {
    r_ssize* v_strides = it->v_strides + axis * n;
    for (r_ssize i = 0; i < n; ++i) {
      v_strides[i] = v_v_strides[i][axis];
    }
  }

  it->dimensionality = rray__run_iterator_axes_coalesce(
    it->v_dimensions,
    it->v_strides,
    n,
    dimensionality
  );

  it->start = 0;
  it->end = it->v_dimensions[0];

  it->n = n;

  for (r_ssize i = 0; i < n; ++i) {
    it->v_loc[i] = 0;
  }

  for (int axis = 0; axis < it->dimensionality; ++axis) {
    it->v_point[axis] = 0;
  }
}

static inline void rray_run_iterator_init1(
  struct rray_run_iterator* it,
  const int* v_dimensions,
  int dimensionality,
  const r_ssize* v_strides
) {
  const r_ssize* v_v_strides[] = {v_strides};
  rray_run_iterator_init(it, v_dimensions, dimensionality, v_v_strides, 1);
}

static inline void rray_run_iterator_init2(
  struct rray_run_iterator* it,
  const int* v_dimensions,
  int dimensionality,
  const r_ssize* v_strides1,
  const r_ssize* v_strides2
) {
  const r_ssize* v_v_strides[] = {v_strides1, v_strides2};
  rray_run_iterator_init(it, v_dimensions, dimensionality, v_v_strides, 2);
}

static inline void rray_run_iterator_reset(struct rray_run_iterator* it) {
  it->start = 0;
  it->end = it->v_dimensions[0];

  for (r_ssize i = 0; i < it->n; ++i) {
    it->v_loc[i] = 0;
  }

  for (int axis = 0; axis < it->dimensionality; ++axis) {
    it->v_point[axis] = 0;
  }
}

static inline void rray_run_iterator_reset1(struct rray_run_iterator* it) {
  it->start = 0;
  it->end = it->v_dimensions[0];

  it->v_loc[0] = 0;

  for (int axis = 0; axis < it->dimensionality; ++axis) {
    it->v_point[axis] = 0;
  }
}

static inline void rray_run_iterator_reset2(struct rray_run_iterator* it) {
  it->start = 0;
  it->end = it->v_dimensions[0];

  it->v_loc[0] = 0;
  it->v_loc[1] = 0;

  for (int axis = 0; axis < it->dimensionality; ++axis) {
    it->v_point[axis] = 0;
  }
}

static inline r_ssize rray_run_iterator_size(
  const struct rray_run_iterator* it
) {
  return it->size;
}

static inline r_ssize rray_run_iterator_start(
  const struct rray_run_iterator* it
) {
  return it->start;
}

static inline r_ssize rray_run_iterator_end(
  const struct rray_run_iterator* it
) {
  return it->end;
}

Thinking

Confirmed: the iterator never splits a run, and when out_stride == 0 the run always spans a full lane, so two-pass is safe there. I'll correct the earlier memory note since part of it was wrong.

Write ~/.claude/projects/-Users-davis-files-r-packages-rray4/memory/project_locate_bitwi… 
---
name: project-locate-bitwise-slower
description: "Locate loops: bitwise helpers only pay off once the across-lanes loop hoists its loads, lane loops need two passes to get fast"
metadata:
  type: project
---

On 2026-10-07, swapping `&&` / `||` for `bool_bitwise_and()` / `bool_bitwise_or()` in the `rray_locate_*_one()` functions in `src/locate.c` as written then was 1.18x to 5.8x slower for int and logical, and neutral for double (192 cases, `-falign-loops=64`). Neither loop vectorized.

Standalone C prototypes (`clang -O2`, 1e7 elements) showed how to make them fast:

- Across lanes (`out_stride != 0`): `v_out[i] = better ? position : v_out[i]` blocks vectorization ("cost model: not beneficial"). Loading `best` and `out` into locals before either store, plus bitwise helpers, vectorizes it: int 17.7 to 2.2 ms, double 22.8 to 3.3 ms. With `&&` it stays slow even when the loads are hoisted.

- Along a lane (`out_stride == 0`): clang has no index-of-max reduction. Two passes (plain max plus a missing flag, then a scalar search for the first equal value) gave int 12.7 to 2.0 ms, and double 39 to 12 ms even though the double max stays scalar. Each such run is always a whole lane.

**Why:** a branch on "`best` is missing" is cheap when the loop is scalar anyway, but it blocks SIMD.

**How to apply:** don't judge bitwise helpers on a loop that can't vectorize for other reasons. Fix the store pattern first. See [[project-loop-alignment-noise]].
The file ~/.claude/projects/-Users-davis-files-r-packages-rray4/memory/project_locate_bitwise_slower.md has been updated successfully.
Edit ~/.claude/projects/-Users-davis-files-r-packages-rray4/memory/MEMORY.md 
-Bitwise and/or helpers slow the locate one functions (int/lgl up to 5.8x), keep `&&` and `||`
+Locate loops vectorize with hoisted loads plus bitwise (across lanes) and two passes (along a lane)
The file ~/.claude/projects/-Users-davis-files-r-packages-rray4/memory/MEMORY.md has been updated successfully.

Yes, there is a way. Each of the two loop shapes needs its own fix. All numbers below come from small C prototypes (clang -O2, 1e7 elements), not from the package yet.

Across lanes (reducing any axis except the first)

This loop can vectorize, but the way it stores results blocks it. The problem is v_out[i] = better ? position : v_out[i]: clang reports "vectorization not beneficial" even for a plain x > best. Two changes fix it:

  1. Load best and out into locals before either store.

  2. Use the bitwise helpers. With &&, the loop stays slow even after step 1.

Now Hoisted + && Hoisted + bitwise
int, 1e6 x 10 17.7 ms 21.7 ms 2.2 ms
int, 10% NA 37.6 ms 20.2 ms 2.2 ms
double 22.8 ms 3.3 ms

So bitwise does pay off, but only once the store pattern is fixed. That's why it lost in the earlier benchmark. Doubles vectorize here too, because each lane is updated on its own, so the NaN rule that blocks double reductions doesn't apply.

Along a lane (reducing axis 1, or a plain vector)

A one-pass "max and its position" loop can't vectorize. clang has no reduction for "index of the max". Two passes work instead:

  1. A plain max plus an "any missing" flag. For ints this vectorizes, using the same pattern as plans/min-max.md.

  2. A scalar loop that finds the first element equal to that max. It's skipped when the lane has a missing value and na_rm = FALSE.

Now Two-pass
int, no NA 12.7 ms 2.0 ms
int, 10% NA 12.7 ms 0.8 ms
int, na_rm = TRUE 3.8 ms 1.8 ms
double 39 ms 12 ms

The double max loop stays scalar, because a compare-and-select reduction on doubles needs -ffast-math and you rejected the int64 key. It still gets 3x faster because a plain max does less work per element than also tracking the position.

Why two-pass gives the same answers:

  • The iterator never splits a run. When out_stride == 0, the run is always the whole lane, so two passes per run are valid.

  • -0 and 0: the search uses ==, so it returns the first of either, which is what happens today.

  • With na_rm = TRUE and every value missing, the search finds nothing and returns NA, also as today.

  • Worst case, the max is the last element, so the scalar search reads the whole lane a second time.

Not yet measured

  • Short lanes, like 10 x 1e6 along axis 1, where the second pass adds overhead to every 10-element lane.

  • The package build with the real iterator and its stride that is only known at run time. My prototype with a run-time stride did vectorize, but it was the && version, which stayed slow.

I updated the memory note from the last round, since "don't use bitwise in locate loops" turned out to be wrong.

This is a fair amount of new kernel code. Do you want it in this PR, or as a separate stacked PR after this one merges?

149	static rray_locate_fn rray_locate_switch(
150	  r_obj* x,
151	  enum rray_locate_op op,
152	  struct rray_arg* arg,
153	  struct r_lazy error_call
154	) {
155	  switch (rray_typeof(x)) {
156	  case RRAY_TYPE_logical:
157	    switch (op) {
158	    case RRAY_LOCATE_max:
159	      return rray_locate_max_lgl;
160	    case RRAY_LOCATE_min:
161	      return rray_locate_min_lgl;
162	    }
163	  case RRAY_TYPE_integer:
164	    switch (op) {
165	    case RRAY_LOCATE_max:
166	      return rray_locate_max_int;
167	    case RRAY_LOCATE_min:
168	      return rray_locate_min_int;
169	    }
170	  case RRAY_TYPE_double:
171	    switch (op) {
172	    case RRAY_LOCATE_max:
173	      return rray_locate_max_dbl;
174	    case RRAY_LOCATE_min:
175	      return rray_locate_min_dbl;
176	    }
177	
178	  case RRAY_TYPE_complex:
179	  case RRAY_TYPE_character:
180	  case RRAY_TYPE_raw:
181	  case RRAY_TYPE_list:
182	    switch (op) {
183	    case RRAY_LOCATE_max:
184	      stop_unsupported_locate("maximum", x, arg, error_call);
185	    case RRAY_LOCATE_min:
186	      stop_unsupported_locate("minimum", x, arg, error_call);
187	    }
188	
189	  case RRAY_TYPE_scalar:
190	    stop_scalar_input(x, arg, error_call);
191	  }
192	
193	  r_stop_unreachable();
194	}
195	
196	static r_no_return void stop_unsupported_locate(
197	  const char* op,
198	  r_obj* x,
199	  struct rray_arg* arg,
200	  struct r_lazy error_call
201	) {
202	  r_abort_lazy_call(
203	    error_call,
204	    "Can't locate the %s of %s.",
205	    op,
206	    rray_arg_type_format(arg, rray_typeof(x))
207	  );
208	}
209

Now I'll write the new kernels. The lane path does two passes using the existing reducers from one-extremum.h. The across path hoists its loads and uses bitwise comparisons.

210	#define RRAY_LOCATE(CTYPE, CONST_DEREF, DEREF, IS_MISSING, INIT, ONE)          \
211	  const CTYPE* v_x = CONST_DEREF(x);                                           \
212	  CTYPE* v_best = DEREF(best);                                                 \
213	                                                                               \
214	  const r_ssize out_size = r_length(best);                                     \
215	                                                                               \
216	  for (r_ssize i = 0; i < out_size; ++i) {                                     \
217	    v_best[i] = INIT;                                                          \
218	    v_out[i] = 1;                                                              \
219	  }                                                                            \
220	                                                                               \
221	  for (; !rray_run_iterator_done(it); rray_run_iterator_next2(it)) {           \
222	    const r_ssize start = rray_run_iterator_start(it);                         \
223	    const r_ssize end = rray_run_iterator_end(it);                             \
224	                                                                               \
225	    r_ssize out_loc = rray_run_iterator_loc(it, 0);                            \
226	    const r_ssize out_stride = rray_run_iterator_stride(it, 0);                \
227	                                                                               \
228	    int position = (int) rray_run_iterator_loc(it, 1) + 1;                     \
229	    const int position_stride = (int) rray_run_iterator_stride(it, 1);         \
230	                                                                               \
231	    if (out_stride == 0) {                                                     \
232	      CTYPE best_elt = v_best[out_loc];                                        \

234	                                                                               \
235	      for (r_ssize i = start; i < end; ++i) {                                  \
236	        const CTYPE x_elt = v_x[i];                                            \
237	        const bool is_better = ONE(x_elt, best_elt);                           \
238	        best_elt = is_better ? x_elt : best_elt;                               \
239	        best_position = is_better ? position : best_position;                  \
240	        position += position_stride;                                           \
241	      }                                                                        \
242	                                                                               \
243	      v_best[out_loc] = best_elt;                                              \
244	      v_out[out_loc] = best_position;                                          \
245	    } else {                                                                   \
246	      for (r_ssize i = start; i < end; ++i) {                                  \
247	        const CTYPE x_elt = v_x[i];                                            \
248	        const bool is_better = ONE(x_elt, v_best[out_loc]);                    \
249	        v_best[out_loc] = is_better ? x_elt : v_best[out_loc];                 \
250	        v_out[out_loc] = is_better ? position : v_out[out_loc];                \
251	        out_loc += out_stride;                                                 \
252	      }                                                                        \
253	    }                                                                          \
254	  }                                                                            \
255	                                                                               \
256	  for (r_ssize i = 0; i < out_size; ++i) {                                     \
257	    v_out[i] = IS_MISSING(v_best[i]) ? r_globals.na_int : v_out[i];            \
258	  }

+#define RRAY_LOCATE_LANE(CTYPE, CONST_DEREF, IS_MISSING, NA_RM, INIT, REDUCE)  \
+  const CTYPE* v_x = CONST_DEREF(x);                                           \
+  int* v_out = r_int_begin(out);                                               \
+                                                                               \
+  for (; !rray_run_iterator_done(it); rray_run_iterator_next2(it)) {           \
+    const r_ssize start = rray_run_iterator_start(it);                         \
+    const r_ssize end = rray_run_iterator_end(it);                             \
+    const r_ssize out_loc = rray_run_iterator_loc(it, 0);                      \
+                                                                               \
+    CTYPE best = INIT;                                                         \
+    bool any_missing = false;                                                  \
+                                                                               \
+    for (r_ssize i = start; i < end; ++i) {                                    \
+      const CTYPE x_elt = v_x[i];                                              \
+      best = REDUCE(best, x_elt);                                              \
+      if (!NA_RM) {                                                            \
+        any_missing = bool_bitwise_or(any_missing, IS_MISSING(x_elt));         \
+      }                                                                        \
+    }                                                                          \
+                                                                               \
+    int out_elt = r_globals.na_int;                                            \
+                                                                               \
+    if (NA_RM || !any_missing) {                                               \
+      int position = (int) rray_run_iterator_loc(it, 1) + 1;                   \
+                                                                               \
+      for (r_ssize i = start; i < end; ++i, ++position) {                      \
+        if (v_x[i] == best) {                                                  \
+          out_elt = position;                                                  \
+          break;                                                               \
+        }                                                                      \
+      }                                                                        \
+    }                                                                          \
+                                                                               \
+    v_out[out_loc] = out_elt;                                                  \
+  }
+
+#define RRAY_LOCATE_ACROSS(                                                    \
+  CTYPE,                                                                       \
+  CONST_DEREF,                                                                 \
+  DEREF,                                                                       \
+  TYPE,                                                                        \
+  IS_MISSING,                                                                  \
+  INIT,                                                                        \
+  ONE                                                                          \
+)                                                                              \
+  const CTYPE* v_x = CONST_DEREF(x);                                           \
+  int* v_out = r_int_begin(out);                                               \
+  const r_ssize out_size = r_length(out);                                      \
+                                                                               \
+  r_obj* best = KEEP(r_alloc_vector(TYPE, out_size));                          \
+  CTYPE* v_best = DEREF(best);                                                 \
+                                                                               \
+  for (r_ssize i = 0; i < out_size; ++i) {                                     \
+    v_best[i] = INIT;                                                          \
+    v_out[i] = 1;                                                              \
+  }                                                                            \
+                                                                               \
+  for (; !rray_run_iterator_done(it); rray_run_iterator_next2(it)) {           \
+    const r_ssize start = rray_run_iterator_start(it);                         \
+    const r_ssize end = rray_run_iterator_end(it);                             \
+                                                                               \
+    r_ssize out_loc = rray_run_iterator_loc(it, 0);                            \
+    const r_ssize out_stride = rray_run_iterator_stride(it, 0);                \
+                                                                               \
+    const int position = (int) rray_run_iterator_loc(it, 1) + 1;               \
+                                                                               \
+    for (r_ssize i = start; i < end; ++i) {                                    \
+      const CTYPE x_elt = v_x[i];                                              \
+      const CTYPE best_elt = v_best[out_loc];                                  \
+      const int out_elt = v_out[out_loc];                                      \
+      const bool is_better = ONE(x_elt, best_elt);                             \
+      v_best[out_loc] = is_better ? x_elt : best_elt;                          \
+      v_out[out_loc] = is_better ? position : out_elt;                         \
+      out_loc += out_stride;                                                   \
+    }                                                                          \
+  }                                                                            \
+                                                                               \
+  for (r_ssize i = 0; i < out_size; ++i) {                                     \
+    v_out[i] = IS_MISSING(v_best[i]) ? r_globals.na_int : v_out[i];            \
+  }                                                                            \
+                                                                               \
+  FREE(1);

Thinking

The across loop isn't vectorizing due to unsafe dependent memory operations, since my prototype assumed a fixed stride of 1 but the real build uses a runtime out_stride. Since that stride is always 1 for the across path, I'll step out_loc by 1 directly instead.

Thinking

All "across" loops vectorize now, and along a lane the max loops vectorize while double loops stay scalar as expected—but int/logical min loops don't, since rray_min_int_one_na_rm() isn't recognized as a min reduction. I'll apply the planned fix: substitute missing values with the starting value and use plain min, falling back to rray_min_int_one() when na_rm isn't set since NA wins there anyway.

Write /tmp/compare-locate-vec.R 
read <- function(label) {
  files <- Sys.glob(sprintf("/tmp/locate-vec-%s-*.csv", label))
  runs <- lapply(files, read.csv)
  out <- runs[[1]]
  out$median_ms <- do.call(pmin, lapply(runs, `[[`, "median_ms"))
  out$build <- NULL
  out
}

base <- read("base")
new <- read("new")
keys <- c("type", "missing", "shape", "op", "na_rm")
both <- merge(base, new, by = keys, suffixes = c("_base", "_new"))
both$ratio <- both$median_ms_new / both$median_ms_base
both$path <- ifelse(grepl("ax1$", both$shape), "lane", "across")

both <- both[order(both$ratio), ]
options(width = 200)
print(both, row.names = FALSE, digits = 3)

geomean <- function(r) exp(mean(log(r)))

cat("\nGeometric mean ratio (new / base) by path and type\n")
print(aggregate(ratio ~ path + type + na_rm, both, geomean), digits = 3)

cat("\nBy shape\n")
print(aggregate(ratio ~ shape + type, both, geomean), digits = 3)

      check = FALSE,
      filter_gc = FALSE
    )

      lane = lane,
      type = type,
      ms = as.numeric(res$median) * 1000
    )
  }
}

Davis

I think the across version looks nice and tidy but I think the lane version is getting too complex. We can’t just use the across version there too? Or something similar to it?

Claude

Short answer: no. Reusing the across loop for lanes just gets back the old, slow loop. Here's why, plus a way to make the lane version simpler.

Why the across loop doesn't carry over

  • The across loop is fast because each element in a run updates a different output slot. The steps don't depend on each other, so clang can do 4 or 16 at a time.

  • Along a lane, every element updates the same slot. Each step has to wait for the previous best, which is exactly the serial loop we started from. The baseline numbers are that loop: 13 to 16 ms for int and 39 ms for double, against 1 to 3 ms and 12 ms now.

The "something similar" option, and why I wouldn't take it

You could cut a lane into 16 interleaved slots, run the across loop over 16-element chunks, then pick the winner among the 16 slots. That does vectorize. But it needs:

  • a slot buffer,

  • a final step that rebuilds the real positions from (chunk, slot) and breaks ties by the smallest position,

  • the same missing-value rules applied again in that final step,

  • and lanes shorter than 16 still get no benefit.

That's more code than what we have now, not less.

What I'd do instead: make the lane macro smaller

Most of its bulk comes from the compile-time NA_RM parameter, which forces two call sites per kernel (12 total). Two changes remove it:

  1. Always compute the missing flag and check na_rm at run time, after the loop. The flag is one extra vector OR per element, which should be close to free, but I'd measure it.

  2. Use one reducer per kernel that skips missing values (rray_max_int_one_na_rm(), or the rray_locate_min_int_lane() mapping for min). Without na_rm, the missing flag decides the result anyway, so which reducer runs doesn't matter.

The macro would then be:

#define RRAY_LOCATE_LANE(CTYPE, CONST_DEREF, IS_MISSING, INIT, REDUCE)
  ...
  for each run {
    CTYPE best = INIT;
    bool any_missing = false;

    for (i in run) {
      best = REDUCE(best, v_x[i]);
      any_missing = bool_bitwise_or(any_missing, IS_MISSING(v_x[i]));
    }

    int out_elt = NA;

    if (na_rm || !any_missing) {
      for (i in run, backwards) {
        out_elt = v_x[i] == best ? position : out_elt;
      }
    }

    v_out[out_loc] = out_elt;
  }

That's one call per kernel, the same as the across path, and five macro parameters instead of six.
